Framed Listening: Sentence Embedding Analyses (individual MIMC level)

Author

Hazel A. van der Walle (PhD student, Music, Durham University)

Published

May 11, 2026

All datasets generated and used for this study are openly available on GitHub: https://github.com/HazelvdW/context-framed-listening.

This document analyses semantic similarity between individual music-influenced mental content (MIMC) descriptions using sentence embeddings and cosine similarity. It is the third pipeline in the analysis chain and the only one where all five condition categories are fully available.

Position in the analysis chain:

Document Method Level N texts RQ2b available?
NLP-TFIDF-analysis.qmd TF-IDF combMIMC (aggregated) 64 No
NLP-SEmb-analysis.qmd (this doc) Sentence embeddings Individual MIMC N per participant Yes

At the individual level, each row is one participant’s MIMC description for one clip × context cell. This means SC_SX pairs (same clip, same context, different participants) now exist and are fully populated, enabling RQ3b (combined alignment advantage) to be tested for the first time in the pipeline.

Model selection note: all-MiniLM-L6-v2 is used as the default model. It was fine-tuned specifically for semantic similarity on diverse sentence-level text and performs strongly on short inputs (3–128 tokens), matching the short length of individual MIMC descriptions. The model name is set in a single config variable below and can be swapped without modifying any other code — see the model comparison note in the setup section for alternatives including mpnet.

Analyses mirror the aggregated combMIMC pipeline in structure, conditions, and research questions exactly.

Sentence Embeddings

Encode each individual MIMC description using the configured model. Embeddings are cached to disk keyed to the model slug so re-runs skip encoding entirely, and switching models automatically triggers a fresh encode.

Loaded cached embeddings: (1960, 384)  (all-MiniLM-L6-v2)

Embedding matrix: 1960 texts × 384 dims
L2 norms (should be ~1.0): min=1.0000  max=1.0000

Pairwise Similarity by Condition

At the individual level the full N×N pairwise matrix is too large to hold in memory if N is large. Similarities are instead computed in batches and written directly to the condition records, avoiding materialising the full matrix.

Each pair is annotated with the same five condition labels as the combMIMC pipeline. Because participants contribute one response per cell, SC_SX pairs now exist: two different participants who heard the same clip under the same context label.

Note on non-independence: pairs at the individual level are not independent — each participant’s response appears in multiple pairs. This is the same structure as the combMIMC pairwise comparisons and is standard in semantic similarity research of this kind. Welch’s t-tests are used as in the combMIMC pipeline; the pair is the unit of analysis.

Condition Description
SC_SX Same clip, same context — different participants — combined alignment
SC_dx Same clip, different context — isolates clip influence
dc_SX_SG Different clip, same context, same genre
dc_dx_SG Different clip, different context, same genre
dc_SX_dg Different clip, same context, different genre — isolates context influence
dc_dx_dg Entirely different — dissimilarity floor (observed lower bound) 
Total unique pairs: 1,919,820

dc_dx_SG     269955
dc_dx_dg    1080315

Linear Mixed Models

Linear mixed models (LMMs) provide a complementary, model-based approach to the pairwise t-tests above. The outcome is pairwise cosine similarity; fixed effects are context_comb (the context labels from the pair) and genre_comb (the genres of items in the pair); item_i and item_j are included as a random intercept to account for the fact that each individual response appears in multiple pairs.

Three models are fitted in sequence. Model 1 tests main effects of context, genre, and clip — clip is included as both a fixed and random effect to separate clip-specific idiosyncrasies from the genre signal, since genre is fully nested within clip. Model 3 adds mean believability (from the supplementary study) as a covariate at the clip × context cell level (64 unique values, one per clip–context pairing) — this gives genuine within-context variation in believability and avoids the collinearity that would arise from collapsing to context-level means. Model 2 tests the context × genre interaction while retaining clip as a covariate. Model comparisons use likelihood ratio tests (ANOVA on nested models) to determine whether each additional term improves fit.

Following the LMMs, estimated marginal means (emmeans) unpack pairwise context differences while controlling for genre. Finally, a Random Forest classifier and a Linear Discriminant Analysis (LDA) are used to assess how well context condition can be recovered from similarity scores alone.

Supplementary Study — Does Believability Moderate the Context Effect?

LMM Model 3 adds pair-level believability as a covariate over the context and genre main effects. This tests whether more believable clip–context pairings produce more semantically similar MIMC, independent of the context and genre structure already captured by Model 1.


———Model 3: similarity ~ context_comb + genre_comb + believability_pair———
  + (1 | clip_comb) + (1 | pid_i) + (1 | pid_j)

RQ1 — Does Context Shape MIMC Similarity?

Logic: Compare dc_SX_dg (different music, same context label) against dc_dx_dg (everything different — the dissimilarity floor). If context drives convergence, pairs sharing a context label should be more similar than pairs sharing nothing.

RQ2 — Does Genre Moderate the Context Effect?

Logic: Two complementary tests. Framing susceptibility: per genre, how much does sharing a context label raise similarity above the dissimilarity floor? Within-genre clip vs context dominance: within each genre, does clip or context drive more convergence? Model 2 (context × genre interaction) is presented here as the model-based complement to these pairwise tests.

RQ3 — Does Music Clip or Context Drive Greater Convergence?

Logic: SC_dx (same clip, different contexts) isolates the music contribution; dc_SX_dg (different clips, same context) isolates the context contribution. RQ3b tests whether aligning both factors (SC_SX) produces additional convergence beyond either alone — this is the first pipeline in the chain where this test is available, as individual-level data provides within-cell pairs across different participants.

Clip vs Context (RQ3a)

RQ3a — CLIP vs CONTEXT
============================================================
  SC_dx (clip-driven):    M = 0.1941  (N = 90,271)
  dc_SX_dg (context-driven): M = 0.1731  (N = 360,086)
  Mean difference = +0.0210,  95% CI [0.0202, 0.0219]
  Welch's t(131846.3) = 48.253,  p = 0.0000  ***
  Cohen's d = 0.184  [note: d benchmarks not appropriate for pairwise data]

-> CLIP drives greater MIMC similarity at the individual level

RQ3b — Combined Advantage: SC_SX vs Single-Factor Conditions

RQ3b — COMBINED ALIGNMENT ADVANTAGE
=================================================================
  SC_SX  M=0.2027  SD=0.1214  N=29,320
  SC_dx  M=0.1941  SD=0.1188  N=90,271
  dc_SX_dg  M=0.1731  SD=0.1102  N=360,086

Welch's t-tests  (Bonferroni k=2,  alpha_adj=0.0250)
-----------------------------------------------------------------

  Combined advantage over Clip-only
    Mean diff = +0.0086,  95% CI [0.0070, 0.0102]
    Welch's t(48862.4) = 10.581  p = 0.0000 ***  ->  p_bonf = 0.0000 ***  d = 0.072

  Combined advantage over Context-only

Exploratory Analyses

The analyses below do not map directly onto the three pre-specified research questions. They are retained as descriptive and methodological supplements that may be useful for characterising the data or for future exploratory work.
